Surah: 91. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

When your discovery is complete and before you write your final output, run this command once, with every Quran reference outside this surah that your output will use: `python3 /Volumes/aro/projects/prose_generation/_commentary/v16/missing.py S91 <refs separated by spaces>`. It lists refs from an earlier cross-reference list, by tier, that your refs do not include. That list is not authoritative and may be incomplete: judge each passage yourself, add it only where it supports or sharpens what you are writing, and leave the rest. To read a passage's Arabic before judging it, run `python3 /Volumes/aro/projects/prose_generation/_commentary/v16/missing.py text <refs>` (up to 40 refs per call) as often as you need. Run each command exactly as written, as the whole command: no cd, no && and no ; (such a command is refused and never runs). Also add any other passage you now recall that belongs, whether listed or not. Then write your final output. No other command or tool is available.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/map3/surah_map.md (adapted) =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not delegate, browse or inspect files; run only the command the header describes.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the surah,
and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear. A member
need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah, not in one
word. A chain may join a sense and its reversal as well as the parts of one scene. Mark a member attested only
inside a fixed expression [fixed expression]; add no other label to a member or a chain, and where a source
records a phrase and rejects it, say so of that phrase alone. When the dictionary itself joins two of the
surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at
the level of its objects, their parts and their operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into
another's more abstract function unless nothing concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test,
however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain; for
   each, name the speaker and the situation in a few words, and include the ayah that opens its scene when the
   passage continues one.
2. `## Interactions`. Where chains meet: a shared member, a dictionary phrase that joins members of two chains,
   a Quran passage that stages two chains together, or one chain's scene needing another's. One line each: the
   chains, where they meet, the evidence.
3. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: the chains its words take part in, what its words add to each, and in one
   line the whole scene each chain makes across the surah, so that a writer who sees only this ayah sees the
   scene, not a fragment.
4. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain: one short line
   each, its name and why, no prose.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.

# Surah 91

- 91:1 وَٱلشَّمْسِ وَضُحَىٰهَا
- 91:2 وَٱلْقَمَرِ إِذَا تَلَىٰهَا
- 91:3 وَٱلنَّهَارِ إِذَا جَلَّىٰهَا
- 91:4 وَٱلَّيْلِ إِذَا يَغْشَىٰهَا
- 91:5 وَٱلسَّمَآءِ وَمَا بَنَىٰهَا
- 91:6 وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَا طَحَىٰهَا
- 91:7 وَنَفْسٍۢ وَمَا سَوَّىٰهَا
- 91:8 فَأَلْهَمَهَا فُجُورَهَا وَتَقْوَىٰهَا
- 91:9 قَدْ أَفْلَحَ مَن زَكَّىٰهَا
- 91:10 وَقَدْ خَابَ مَن دَسَّىٰهَا
- 91:11 كَذَّبَتْ ثَمُودُ بِطَغْوَىٰهَآ
- 91:12 إِذِ ٱنۢبَعَثَ أَشْقَىٰهَا
- 91:13 فَقَالَ لَهُمْ رَسُولُ ٱللَّهِ نَاقَةَ ٱللَّهِ وَسُقْيَٰهَا
- 91:14 فَكَذَّبُوهُ فَعَقَرُوهَا فَدَمْدَمَ عَلَيْهِمْ رَبُّهُم بِذَنۢبِهِمْ فَسَوَّىٰهَا
- 91:15 وَلَا يَخَافُ عُقْبَٰهَا

# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

===== _commentary/v16/work/s091/surah.r2.hftbundle/channels.md =====
(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s091/reader_a_pilot.md)

# S091 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Cycles of Exposure and Occlusion
- Semantic invariant: A field becomes visible, bright, veiled, or perceptually inaccessible through recurring changes of light and covering.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:1-4 moves from `الشمس` and `ضحاها` through the moon that `تلاها`, the day that `جلاها`, and the night that `يغشاها`.
- Surprising reach: The cycle extends from celestial alternation into polishing, glare, white markings, enveloping calamity, and loss of awareness.

#### Subchannel A. Celestial Alternation
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Sun, moon, day, and night succeed one another as illumination advances and covering returns.
- Active motifs: solar disc and light `ش م س:B001/m01`; forenoon interval `ض ح و:B001/m01`; moon and moonlight `ق م ر:B001/m01`; full-moon night `س و ي:B012/m01`; bodily or ranked following `ت ل و:B001/m01`; bright day `ن ه ر:B002/m01`; daylight disclosure `ج ل و:B007/m01`; night darkness `ل ي ل:B001/m01`; covering veil `غ ش و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس` and `ضحاها`; 91:2 `القمر` and `تلاها`; 91:3 `النهار` and `جلاها`; 91:4 `الليل` and `يغشاها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`
- Synthesis: The lights are not static objects but participants in an ordered relay: one follows, another exposes, and another covers. The lexical senses preserve the same alternation as a movement between visible brightness and enveloping darkness.

#### Subchannel B. Dawn as Aperture and Breath
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Darkness or constriction opens into dawn, widened space, and an outward breath.
- Active motifs: dawn breaking from night `ف ج ر:B002/m01`; broad fissure and water-burst `ف ج ر:B001/m01`; opening and widening `ن ه ر:B003/m01`; dawn breathing outward `ن ف س:B009/m01`; public emergence into sunlight `ض ح و:B002/m01`; disclosure after concealment `ج ل و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `ضحاها`; 91:3 `النهار` and `جلاها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:8 `فجورها`
- Synthesis: Dawn is rendered as an aperture: light, space, water, and breath all move outward from confinement. The scene turns temporal emergence into a physical process of splitting, widening, and exhalation.

#### Subchannel C. Polishing, Glare, and Visible Marking
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A surface is brightened until it becomes conspicuous, while excessive whiteness can overwhelm sight.
- Active motifs: polishing and visual clarification `ج ل و:B002/m01`; exposed forehead `ج ل و:B005/m01`; clear white day `ج ل و:B007/m01`; forenoon radiance `ض ح و:B005/m01`; pale or white coloration `ق م ر:B002/m01`; snow-glare confusion `ق م ر:B005/m01`; white facial covering `غ ش و:B007/m01`; white head or face marking `غ ش ي:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس` and `ضحاها`; 91:2 `القمر`; 91:3 `جلاها`; 91:4 `يغشاها`
- Synthesis: Polishing and daylight make forms legible, and white markings make an animal immediately visible. At the limit, the same brightness reverses into snow glare and confused vision, so disclosure and perceptual loss occupy opposite ends of one luminous process.

#### Subchannel D. Veiling and Loss of Awareness
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A covering descends over an object, a population, or consciousness itself.
- Active motifs: material or perceptual covering `غ ش و:B001/m01` and `غ ش ي:B001/m01`; all-enveloping affliction `غ ش و:B002/m01`; swoon or deathly obscuration `غ ش و:B005/m01`; loss of understanding `غ ش ي:B007/m01`; stealthy self-concealment `د س و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:10 `دساها`
- Synthesis: The night’s covering expands into a general mechanism of occlusion. Cloth, disaster, fainting, and deliberate hiding each place a layer between a subject and visibility, awareness, or social notice.

### 2. Formed Space and Vertical Order
- Semantic invariant: Space becomes habitable or intelligible through construction, leveling, spreading, elevation, and stable support.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:5-7 names the sky that was `بناها`, the earth that was `طحاها`, and the self that was `سواها`.
- Surprising reach: Cosmic architecture continues into mountain ridges, smooth plains, house posts, hide shelters, and the base on which property and settlement stand.

#### Subchannel A. Cosmic Architecture
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: An overhead structure and a lower expanse are assembled, spread, and brought into finished form.
- Active motifs: building by joined components `ب ن ي:B001/m01`; sky or overhead canopy `س م و:B004/m01`; lower earth opposite the sky `ء ر ض:B001/m01`; spreading and extension `ط ح و:B001/m01`; straightened completed form `س و ي:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `السماء` and `بناها`; 91:6 `الأرض` and `طحاها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`
- Synthesis: Building, spreading, and leveling form a single spatial operation. The sky supplies the raised canopy, the earth the extended lower field, and leveling the finishing action that gives the whole a coherent constitution.

#### Subchannel B. Level Expanse and Open Way
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Ground is flattened into a broad, fair, traversable interval.
- Active motifs: wide smooth ground `س و ي:B009/m01`; fair middle or level place `س و ي:B006/m01`; earth-spreading `ط ح و:B001/m01`; spacious interval and distance `ن ف س:B015/m01`; gap between two sides `ع ق ر:B014/m01`; thick spread carpet `ء ر ض:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:6 `الأرض` and `طحاها`; 91:7 `نفس` and `سواها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Flattening does more than enlarge the ground: it establishes a middle, a gap, and enough interval for passage or settlement. The carpet image miniaturizes the same operation as a portable plane laid out beneath occupants.

#### Subchannel C. Ascent, Ridge, and Summit
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler or visible figure rises from level ground through a pass toward a ridge or summit.
- Active motifs: rising and eminence `س م و:B001/m01`; high visible figure `س م و:B002/m01`; rising and settling above `س و ي:B003/m01`; elevated moon `ط ح و:B008/m01`; high rock `ط غ و:B006/m01`; smooth high slab `ط غ ي:B005/m01`; mountain summit `ن و ق:B001/m01`; climbable long ridges `ش ق و:B004/m01` and `ش ق ي:B004/m01`; steep mountain pass `ع ق ب:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:12 `أشقاها`; 91:13 `ناقة`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Elevation is distributed across figure, motion, terrain, and endpoint. The pass and ridge make ascent a bodily route, while the summit, high slab, and raised celestial body make height a stable state of visibility and dominance.

#### Subchannel D. Structural Supports and Settlement
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Posts, ribs, bases, and enclosing structures hold a dwelling or settled property in place.
- Active motifs: ribs and house posts `ب ن ي:B009/m01`; hide shelter `ب ن ي:B004/m01`; dwelling base or interior site `ع ق ر:B013/m01`; fortified refuge `ع ق ر:B015/m01`; landed property and household goods `ع ق ر:B016/m01`; staying and dwelling `ر ب ب:B007/m01`; earthbound staying `ء ر ض:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:14 `عقروها` and `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Support begins with load-bearing ribs and posts, expands into a shelter, and terminates in the base, refuge, and property of settlement. Bodily and architectural frames share the same function: they keep an inhabited structure upright and located.

### 3. Release, Flow, and Cultivation
- Semantic invariant: Something confined or still is opened, sent outward, or guided through a channel into productive motion.
- Surface relation: indirect; the roots occur at 91:3 `النهار`, 91:6 `طحاها`, 91:8 `فجورها`, 91:12 `انبعث`, and 91:13 `رسول` and `سقياها`.
- Surprising reach: The same release mechanism joins dispatch, river incision, exhalation, relief from distress, and the opening of soil for cultivation.

#### Subchannel A. Rousing and Dispatch
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A resting participant is stirred, directed, and sent toward a task or destination.
- Active motifs: rousing the still `ب ع ث:B001/m01`; setting out rapidly `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; release and dispatch `ر س ل:B001/m01`; successive movement `ت ل و:B001/m01`; hired carrier or cultivator `ف ل ح:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:9 `أفلح`; 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `رسول`
- Synthesis: Rousing converts rest into directed motion, while dispatch gives that motion a destination and function. Following then turns a single departure into an ordered sequence of participants.

#### Subchannel B. Fissure, Channel, and Overflow
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pressure opens a fissure, water enters a channel, and excess rises beyond its bounds.
- Active motifs: broad opening and water eruption `ف ج ر:B001/m01`; river cutting the earth `ن ه ر:B001/m01`; widened opening `ن ه ر:B003/m01`; irrigation course `س ق ي:B003/m01`; water channels at valley ends `ذ ن ب:B004/m01`; surging overflow `ط غ و:B002/m01` and `ط غ ي:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `النهار`; 91:8 `فجورها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم`
- Synthesis: The channel is a managed fissure: opening allows flow, a course directs it, and overflow marks the point at which directed movement escapes measure. Tail-like valley channels give the process a branching terminal geometry.

#### Subchannel C. Irrigated Ground and Growth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fertile ground is split, watered, covered by low growth, and brought into increase.
- Active motifs: fertile productive soil `ء ر ض:B002/m01`; ploughing and soil-splitting `ف ل ح:B003/m01`; irrigation share `س ق ي:B003/m01`; prostrate ground-cover `ط ح و:B006/m01`; vegetal increase `ز ك و:B001/m01`; persistent green plant `ر ب ب:B012/m01`; tail-shaped plant `ذ ن ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:6 `الأرض` and `طحاها`; 91:9 `زكاها` and `أفلح`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Cultivation coordinates soil, incision, water allotment, and growth. The plants remain close to the earth or branch into tail-like forms, making agricultural increase a visible consequence of correctly guided flow.

#### Subchannel D. Breath and Relief from Constriction
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Breath, space, or assistance opens a constricted condition and restores movement.
- Active motifs: outward breath `ن ف س:B001/m01`; relief of distress `ن ف س:B002/m01`; spaciousness and respite `ن ف س:B015/m01`; easy unforced movement `ر س ل:B003/m01`; familiar ease `ر س ل:B007/m01`; widening and opening `ط ح و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:13 `رسول`
- Synthesis: Exhalation is the bodily form of release, while respite and familiarity are its social and emotional forms. Smooth movement and widening complete a shared passage from pressure into room, ease, and renewed motion.

#### Subchannel E. Rain Cloud and Overcast
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Clouds gather overhead, carry heavy precipitation, obscure the sun, and feed the waters below.
- Active motifs: cloud and rain overhead `س م و:B004/m02`; layered rain cloud `ر ب ب:B008/m01`; heavy rain-bearing cloud `س ق ي:B007/m01`; cloud mass `ن ه ر:B008/m01`; cloud obscuring the sun `ع ق ر:B018/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `النهار`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `ربهم` and `عقروها`
- Synthesis: The cloud scene connects elevation to hydrology. Vapor gathers into an overhead mass, darkens the luminous field, and releases the precipitation that replenishes channels, fields, and drinking supply.

### 4. Abundance, Nourishment, and Giving
- Semantic invariant: A supply exceeds immediate need and becomes sustaining water, food, multitude, wealth, or voluntary benefaction.
- Surface relation: indirect; the relevant roots appear at 91:3 `النهار`, 91:5 `السماء`, 91:8 `فجورها`, 91:9 `زكاها`, 91:13 `سقياها`, and 91:14 `ربهم`.
- Surprising reach: Material plenty passes into abundant milk, a body built by food, large crowds, open-handed giving, and praise spreading among people.

#### Subchannel A. Water, Drink, and Milk
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water or milk is made available, drawn, drunk, and retained as a life-sustaining supply.
- Active motifs: giving drink `س ق ي:B001/m01`; establishing a water supply `س ق ي:B002/m01`; abundant fresh water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`; water sustaining life `ن ف س:B008/m01`; draught or drinking interval `ن ف س:B006/m01`; continuous milk yield `ر س ل:B006/m01`; full water bucket `ذ ن ب:B007/m01`; ladle `ذ ن ب:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 `نفس`; 91:13 `رسول`, `سقياها`, and `ناقة`; 91:14 `ربهم` and `بذنبهم`
- Synthesis: Provision forms a complete handling scene: a source is established, liquid is drawn with bucket and ladle, and the drinker receives sustaining water or milk. Continuity of supply, rather than a single sip, defines the channel.

#### Subchannel B. Food Building the Body
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Ingestion becomes bodily substance, growth, and renewed strength.
- Active motifs: swallowing and consuming `ل ه م:B001/m01`; food building flesh `ب ن ي:B010/m01`; growth and fattening `ز ك و:B001/m01`; thick syrup or nutritive paste `ر ب ب:B006/m01`; giving drink `س ق ي:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:8 `فألهمها`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: The body is treated as something constructed by intake. Swallowing admits material, food and drink supply it, and growth registers the successful conversion of nourishment into flesh and strength.

#### Subchannel C. Multitude and Material Plenty
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water, pasture, livestock, troops, or people accumulate into a conspicuous mass.
- Active motifs: vast crowd or army `ط ح و:B005/m01`; great quantity and scale `ل ه م:B004/m01`; gathered multitudes `ر ب ب:B004/m01`; plentiful water or pasture `ق م ر:B008/m01`; successive herds `ر س ل:B005/m01`; sudden arriving multitude `ف ج ر:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:8 `فجورها` and `فألهمها`; 91:13 `رسول`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Abundance is spatial before it is evaluative: a mass spreads, gathers, or arrives in waves. Water, pasture, animals, and people share the same image of quantity becoming large enough to shape the surrounding scene.

#### Subchannel D. Generosity and Benefaction
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Internal largeness becomes willing giving, bounty, and public good repute.
- Active motifs: gushing generosity `ف ج ر:B005/m01`; generous sufficiency `ل ه م:B004/m02`; willing gift `ر س ل:B010/m02`; generous high spirit `ن ف س:B014/m02`; blessing or benefaction `ر ب ب:B016/m01`; good reputation `س م و:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `السماء`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:8 `فجورها` and `فألهمها`; 91:13 `رسول`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Giving is pictured as overflow from an ample interior. Bounty moves outward with the force of a spring, sustains others, and returns socially as a good name carried beyond the giver.

### 5. Inner Formation and Moral Outcome
- Semantic invariant: The inner person is formed through cognition, protection, purification, concealment, or corruption, and those operations issue in flourishing or failure.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:7-10 moves from the self that was `سواها` through `فجورها وتقواها` to the one who `زكاها` or `دساها`.
- Surprising reach: Moral interiority is rendered through concrete acts of straightening, shielding, burying, breathing, and building.

#### Subchannel A. Self, Life, and Inner Cognition
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A living self possesses identity, awareness, intention, and an inward saying before outward action.
- Active motifs: living soul `ن ف س:B011/m01`; individual self `ن ف س:B012/m01`; cognitive discernment `ن ف س:B013/m01`; completed formation `س و ي:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 `نفس` and `سواها`; 91:14 `فسواها`
- Synthesis: The self is simultaneously life, identity, cognition, and intention. Formation gives this interior a coherent shape from which conduct can proceed.

#### Subchannel B. Purification, Protection, and Flourishing
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: The self is cleansed, guarded from harm, brought into rectitude, and carried toward success.
- Active motifs: purification and righteousness `ز ك و:B002/m01`; self-protective piety `و ق ي:B002/m01`; straightening and sound formation `س و ي:B002/m02`; nurture toward completion `ر ب ب:B002/m01`; success and survival `ف ل ح:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 `سواها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:9 `أفلح` and `زكاها`; 91:14 `ربهم` and `فسواها`
- Synthesis: Purification is not merely removal of stain; it is a constructive regimen of guarding, straightening, and nurture. Its outcome is a self able to endure and flourish.

#### Subchannel C. Concealment, Corruption, and Failure
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: The self is buried from view, turned away from right order, and deprived of its sought outcome.
- Active motifs: stealthy burial `د س و:B001/m01`; misguidance and corruption `د س و:B003/m01`; breach of moral bounds `ف ج ر:B004/m01`; failure and deprivation `خ ي ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:8 `فجورها`; 91:10 `خاب` and `دساها`
- Synthesis: Burial provides the governing image: what should be formed and purified is instead pushed down and obscured. Corruption redirects it, moral breach opens a path away from right order, and failure names the resulting deprivation.

#### Subchannel D. Deceptive Impulse and Divided Interior
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An inner impulse conceals itself, fabricates a claim, and competes with reflective intention.
- Active motifs: deceitful self `ك ذ ب:B008/m01`; concealed inward impulse `د س و:B001/m01`; inwardly drawn saying `ق و ل:B006/m01`; inward intention `ن ف س:B013/m02`; desire for the precious `ن ف س:B010/m01`; protective restraint `و ق ي:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 `نفس`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:10 `دساها`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:13 `قال`
- Synthesis: The interior is a contested space rather than a single voice. Desire and a concealed impulse generate a private account, while intention and restraint determine whether that account remains deceptive or is brought under moral protection.

### 6. Message, Speech, and Social Circulation
- Semantic invariant: Meaning moves from inner conception through an authorized speaker into utterance, response, report, attribution, and public reputation.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:11-14 joins communal denial with the messenger who `قال` and the people who `كذبوه`.
- Surprising reach: Speech extends into private belief, negotiated judgment, musical responsion, rumor, and public reputation.

#### Subchannel A. Messenger and Utterance
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A sender appoints a messenger who bears and voices a statement to an audience.
- Active motifs: dispatched envoy `ب ع ث:B002/m01`; messenger and carried message `ر س ل:B002/m01`; spoken utterance `ق و ل:B001/m01`; tongue as articulator `ق و ل:B002/m01`; authoritative speaker `ق و ل:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `فقال` and `رسول`
- Synthesis: Dispatch establishes provenance, the messenger carries the communication, and utterance makes it public. The authoritative speaker motif gives the voiced message social force beyond its bare wording.

#### Subchannel B. Inner Saying and Belief
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A saying is formed inwardly, aligned with intention, adopted as a belief, and then made available for utterance.
- Active motifs: inward saying `ق و ل:B012/m01`; inwardly drawn speech `ق و ل:B006/m01`; belief or doctrine `ق و ل:B013/m01`; inward intention `ن ف س:B013/m02`; spoken utterance `ق و ل:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 `نفس`; 91:13 `قال`
- Synthesis: Speech begins before sound as an inwardly held conception. Intention gives the conception direction, belief fixes it as a position, and utterance carries it from private thought into an audible statement.

#### Subchannel C. False Attribution and Denial
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A speaker fabricates words, assigns them to another, or discredits a truthful address.
- Active motifs: false report against another `ت ل و:B009/m01`; fabricated attribution `ق و ل:B005/m01`; falsehood `ك ذ ب:B001/m01`; imputing a lie `ك ذ ب:B002/m01`; deceptive embellished statement `ف ل ح:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:9 `أفلح`; 91:11 `كذبت`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:14 `كذبوه`
- Synthesis: Falsehood operates through attribution as well as content. A statement can be invented, placed in another’s mouth, used to discredit a messenger, or ornamented until its persuasive surface masks its invalidity.

#### Subchannel D. Directive Speech, Rebuke, and Objection
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Speech binds an addressee, warns or rebukes, and then becomes subject to objection or rebuttal.
- Active motifs: idiomatic injunction `ك ذ ب:B003/m01`; imposed judgment `ق و ل:B010/m01`; authoritative negotiation `ق و ل:B009/m01`; warning through induced fear `خ و ف:B002/m01`; forceful rebuke `ن ه ر:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `النهار`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:15 `يخاف` and `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Directive discourse moves from command to enforcement: an authority states an obligation, warning gives it urgency, and rebuke confronts refusal. Negotiation then tests how the directive will operate between speakers.

#### Subchannel E. Rumor, Praise, and Reputation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Repeated sayings circulate until they become a person’s public name or standing.
- Active motifs: circulating saying `ق و ل:B007/m01`; good repute `س م و:B008/m01`; fame that cannot be hidden `ج ل و:B006/m01`; loquacious speaker `ق و ل:B003/m01`; strength of character `ن ف س:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `جلاها`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:13 `قال`
- Synthesis: Speech leaves the immediate exchange and travels socially. Repetition makes a report widespread, loquacity increases its reach, and accumulated talk hardens into fame, praise, or moral reputation.

#### Subchannel F. Responsorial Voice and Performance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: One voice calls or performs and another follows, accompanies, or answers it.
- Active motifs: answering voice after a singer `ت ل و:B007/m01`; paired performer or correspondent `ر س ل:B008/m01`; raised singing, reading, or lament `ع ق ر:B009/m01`; spoken articulation `ق و ل:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:13 `قال` and `رسول`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Sequence becomes audible as responsion: a leading sound is followed by a second voice, and paired performers sustain the exchange. Raised recitation, song, and lament give the scene a public, embodied acoustic form.

### 7. Sequence, Remainder, and Consequence
- Semantic invariant: What comes after may follow, replace, remain as a trace, reverse course, or arrive as the consequence of an act.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:2 has the moon that `تلاها`, 91:14 places destruction `بذنبهم`, and 91:15 names `عقباها`.
- Surprising reach: Temporal order is embodied in heels and tails, then extended into residue, retreat, punishment, and repeated action.

#### Subchannel A. Following, Procession, and Iteration
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Participants move one after another, replace predecessors, or repeat a prior action.
- Active motifs: direct following `ت ل و:B001/m01`; successive groups `ر س ل:B005/m01`; succession and replacement `ع ق ب:B005/m01`; repeated return to an action `ع ق ب:B009/m01`; followers behind leaders `ذ ن ب:B003/m01`; rapid collective setting-out `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; immediate action without delay `ك ذ ب:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:11 `كذبت`; 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `رسول`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `كذبوه`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Sequence can be linear, substitutive, or iterative. A follower occupies the wake of another, successive groups preserve the line, and repetition turns one passage into a recurring cycle.

#### Subchannel B. Heel, Tail, Trace, and Remainder
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The rear of a moving body becomes the place where traces and remnants persist.
- Active motifs: heel and footprint `ع ق ب:B002/m01`; tail or terminal rear `ذ ن ب:B002/m02`; remaining portion `ت ل و:B003/m01`; residue and aftereffect `ع ق ب:B011/m01`; last or final part `ع ق ب:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Rear anatomy supplies a concrete model of aftermath. Heel and tail mark the path behind a body, while residue and remainder are what an event leaves in the same trailing position.

#### Subchannel C. Retreat, Desertion, and Interrupted Motion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Forward movement is abandoned, reversed, or broken by a sudden halt.
- Active motifs: abandonment after companionship `ت ل و:B005/m01`; turning back on the heel `ع ق ب:B003/m01`; failed charge or cowardly stop `ك ذ ب:B004/m01`; animal running then halting `ك ذ ب:B007/m01`; withholding after apparent availability `ش م س:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Each motif begins with an expected continuation and then violates it. Companion, attacker, animal, or provider withdraws from the established line, making reversal a social as well as kinetic break.

#### Subchannel D. Sin, Outcome, and Punishment
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: An offense is followed by its terminal result and punitive repayment.
- Active motifs: sin or culpable act `ذ ن ب:B001/m01`; allotted share of punishment `ذ ن ب:B006/m01`; consequence and final outcome `ع ق ب:B006/m01`; punishment after wrongdoing `ع ق ب:B007/m01`; annihilating retribution `د م د م:B001/m01`; fear of an expected harm `خ و ف:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:14 `دمدم`, `بذنبهم`, and `فسواها`; 91:15 `يخاف عقباها`
- Synthesis: Wrongdoing initiates a causal sequence whose last member is not arbitrary: consequence follows the offense as its allotted share. Punishment closes the sequence through total leveling, while fear anticipates that terminal outcome.

### 8. Transgression, Contest, and Collective Force
- Semantic invariant: Force crosses a limit, confronts an opponent, competes for dominance, gathers collectively, and may culminate in destructive overpowering.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:8 names `فجورها`, 91:11 `طغواها`, 91:12 the rising of `أشقاها`, and 91:14 the collective denial, hamstringing, and destruction.
- Surprising reach: Moral rebellion shares a shape with hydraulic overflow, animal restiveness, boasting contests, crowd pressure, and sudden mass arrival.

#### Subchannel A. Rebellion and Boundary Crossing
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A participant exceeds a rightful measure, rejects restraint, and becomes a source of misguidance.
- Active motifs: transgression beyond bounds `ط غ ي:B001/m01`; rebellious excess `ط غ و:B001/m01`; heads of misguidance `ط غ و:B003/m01` and `ط غ ي:B003/m01`; coercive tyrant `ط غ و:B004/m01`; moral breach `ف ج ر:B004/m01`; sacrilegious conflict `ف ج ر:B006/m01`; induced corruption `د س و:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:8 `فجورها`; 91:10 `دساها`; 91:11 `طغواها`
- Synthesis: Transgression is a failure of measure that becomes socially active. The rebel crosses a boundary, the head of misguidance redirects others, and the tyrant converts excess into coercive rule.

#### Subchannel B. Hostility and Confrontation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Antagonism is displayed, an opponent is confronted, and hostile pressure is sustained by words.
- Active motifs: manifest hostility `ش م س:B003/m01`; confronting or exposing oneself to another `ء ر ض:B007/m01`; filling a heart with enmity `س ق ي:B010/m01`; verbal rivalry and insult `ع ق ر:B011/m01`; blows laid on with whip or sword `غ ش و:B006/m01` and `غ ش ي:B006/m01`; harsh rebuke `ن ه ر:B004/m01`; abandonment of an ally `ت ل و:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:3 `النهار`; 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Hostility moves from inward saturation to outward display. Confrontation exposes the adversaries to one another, verbal rivalry keeps the conflict active, and desertion changes the social alignment around it.

#### Subchannel C. Competition and Overcoming
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Rivals test endurance, fear, prestige, or skill until one overcomes the other.
- Active motifs: contest in fear `خ و ف:B003/m01`; rivalry and boasting `س م و:B007/m01`; deceptive victory in play `ق م ر:B007/m02`; negotiated contest `ق و ل:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:12 `أشقاها`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:15 `يخاف`
- Synthesis: Competition measures relative capacity rather than absolute strength. Fear, prestige, strategy, and speech become different arenas in which one participant seeks to exceed another.

#### Subchannel D. Hardship, Endurance, and Overcoming
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Participants enter a difficult struggle, endure its strain, and seek to outlast or overcome one another.
- Active motifs: wretched state `ش ق ي:B001/m01`; hardship and exertion `ش ق و:B002/m01` and `ش ق ي:B002/m01`; mutual struggle `ش ق ي:B003/m01`; overcoming in struggle `ش ق و:B003/m01`; difficult temperament `ش م س:B002/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:12 `أشقاها`
- Synthesis: Hardship supplies both the condition and the arena of contest. Endurance keeps a participant within the struggle, difficult temperament intensifies resistance, and overcoming names the point at which relative stamina becomes dominance.

#### Subchannel E. Crowd, Rush, and Jostling
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A mass gathers, surges forward, pushes internally, and becomes audible as a collective force.
- Active motifs: rapid collective movement `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; sudden arrival of a multitude `ف ج ر:B003/m01`; mutual shoving `ط ح و:B004/m01`; crowd or army of great extent `ط ح و:B005/m01`; collective voice `ط غ و:B008/m01`; circulating public saying `ق و ل:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:8 `فجورها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `قال`
- Synthesis: Collective force has spatial, kinetic, and acoustic phases. A crowd arrives, occupies breadth, presses against itself, and produces a voice or report proportionate to its scale.

#### Subchannel F. Seizure and Extirpation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Sudden force snatches, crushes, or engulfs until the target is erased.
- Active motifs: total extirpation `د م د م:B001/m01`; sudden snatch `ن ه ر:B006/m01`; engulfing calamity `غ ش و:B002/m01`; devouring disaster `ل ه م:B005/m01`; overpowering punishment `ط غ و:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `النهار`; 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:8 `فألهمها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:14 `دمدم`
- Synthesis: Destruction is not merely a final state but a mechanism of sudden enclosure and removal. Snatching initiates it, engulfment denies escape, and extirpation leaves no surviving differentiation.

### 9. Animal Movement and Wild Ecology
- Semantic invariant: Animals are roused, ridden, restrained, injured, herded, hunted, fed, or located within a habitat structured by water and vegetation.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:12-14 moves from `انبعث` to `ناقة الله وسقياها` and then `عقروها`.
- Surprising reach: The she-camel scene opens into riding technology, restive temperament, moonlit hunting, wetland birds, herd succession, and nocturnal feeding routines.

#### Subchannel A. Camel Handling and Riding
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A resting camel is roused, tamed, equipped, and guided into an easy gait.
- Active motifs: rousing a resting camel `ب ع ث:B001/m01`; she-camel form `ن و ق:B002/m01`; taming and management `ن و ق:B004/m01`; easy gait `ر س ل:B003/m01`; riding pad `س و ي:B010/m01`; saddle covering `غ ش و:B001/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `رسول` and `ناقة`
- Synthesis: Management transforms a resting or difficult animal into a rideable participant. Taming governs behavior, pad and cover distribute the rider’s load, and easy gait is the successful outcome of equipment and handling.

#### Subchannel B. Restiveness, Hoof Protection, and Immobilization
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A skittish animal resists mounting, guards an injured hoof, or becomes unable to move.
- Active motifs: restive animal `ش م س:B002/m01`; immobilizing saddle injury or shock `ع ق ر:B003/m01`; guarded hoof and slight lameness `و ق ي:B003/m01`; tail and hindpart `ذ ن ب:B002/m01`; heel tendon `ع ق ب:B001/m01`; non-wounding saddle `و ق ي:B003/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `عقروها`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Restiveness and pain are read through altered movement. The animal withholds its back, protects the hoof, or loses locomotion entirely; careful saddle design provides the counter-mechanism that prevents injury.

#### Subchannel C. Hamstringing and Bodily Wound
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A leg or tendon is cut, the animal falls, and slaughter or lasting injury follows.
- Active motifs: cutting the legs of a mount `ع ق ر:B002/m01`; bodily wound `ع ق ر:B001/m01`; heel tendon `ع ق ب:B001/m01`; animal hindpart `ذ ن ب:B002/m01`; she-camel `ن و ق:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:13 `ناقة`; 91:14 `عقروها` and `بذنبهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: The act targets the animal’s locomotive structure. Hindpart, heel tendon, and cut legs form a precise injury scene in which disabling movement becomes the immediate path to the animal’s death.

#### Subchannel D. Moonlit Pursuit and Wild Herds
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters enter moonlight, scan for game, and pursue animals that run, halt, or move in herds.
- Active motifs: setting out to hunt `س م و:B006/m01`; moonlit hunting `ق م ر:B003/m01`; searching raised gaze `ج ل و:B008/m01`; ground-devouring run `ل ه م:B003/m01`; wild cattle herd `ر ب ب:B014/m01`; young wild ox `ط غ و:B009/m01`; game animal running then stopping `ك ذ ب:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:3 `جلاها`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:8 `فألهمها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:14 `ربهم` and `كذبوه`
- Synthesis: Moonlight defines both opportunity and risk: it reveals game while affecting sight. The hunter scans, the herd moves, and the pursued animal’s run-and-halt pattern creates the moment on which capture depends.

#### Subchannel E. Raptors, Carrion, and Wetland Birds
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Birds occupy distinct ecological roles around carrion, prey, reeds, and water.
- Active motifs: eagle or raptor `ع ق ب:B013/m01`; male partridge `ع ق ب:B014/m02`; vultures circling a kill `ط ح و:B003/m01`; shrike `و ق ي:B005/m01`; turtledove `ق م ر:B012/m01`; water-fed papyrus or cane `س ق ي:B008/m01`; abundant water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: The bird class resolves into habitats and feeding roles rather than a bare taxonomy. Raptors and vultures occupy pursuit and carrion scenes, while shrike and turtledove connect to water-fed cane and the margins of a wet habitat.

#### Subchannel F. Feeding, Grazing, and Night Watch
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Herds graze by day, receive a delayed evening feed, and may be watched or left unattended under moonlight.
- Active motifs: forenoon grazing `ض ح و:B003/m02`; delayed camel supper `ق م ر:B010/m01`; unattended livestock by moonlight `ق م ر:B011/m01`; moonlit insomnia `ق م ر:B009/m01`; night activity or travel `ل ي ل:B002/m01`; herd succession `ر س ل:B005/m01`; plentiful pasture `ق م ر:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `ضحاها`; 91:2 `القمر`; 91:4 `الليل`; 91:13 `رسول`
- Synthesis: The herd’s day is organized by feeding intervals and visibility. Forenoon grazing, evening delay, and moonlit watch form a practical cycle in which pasture, timing, and human supervision regulate animal survival.

#### Subchannel G. Mounting, Dominance, and Category Reversal
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A dominant animal mounts, a rider settles above a mount, and a proverb reverses the expected sexed category.
- Active motifs: stallion mounting the herd `س م و:B003/m01`; mounting and settling above `س و ي:B003/m02`; camel becoming like a she-camel in proverb `ن و ق:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `السماء`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:13 `ناقة`
- Synthesis: Vertical position carries social force in the animal scene: the animal above dominates, while the rider above controls. The camel-to-she-camel proverb exposes the instability of that expected role by turning category itself into the reversal.

### 10. Kinship, Development, and Marital Exchange
- Semantic invariant: Persons enter households through birth, nurture, descent, courtship, marriage, or compensatory exchange.
- Surface relation: indirect; the participating roots occur at 91:5 `بناها`, 91:7 `نفس`, 91:13 `رسول` and `ناقة`, 91:14 `عقروها`, and 91:15 `عقباها`.
- Surprising reach: Genealogical following extends to fosterage and infertility, while unveiling and gifts turn visibility into a structured marital transition.

#### Subchannel A. Birth, Fosterage, and Descent
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A child is born, follows the mother, is nurtured by a household, and continues a line of descent.
- Active motifs: birth and newborn `ن ف س:B005/m01`; young following its mother `ت ل و:B006/m01`; sonship and descent `ب ن ي:B007/m01`; small derivative offshoot `ب ن ي:B008/m01`; fostered child `ر ب ب:B005/m01`; caregiver or nurse `ر ب ب:B005/m02`; descendants `ع ق ب:B004/m01`; newborn or lactating animal `ر ب ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:5 `بناها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Birth begins a sequence of dependence and continuation. The young follows the mother, nurture incorporates it into a household, and descent carries that household forward beyond the present generation.

#### Subchannel B. Maturity, Infertility, and Final Offspring
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Development reaches completion, fails to produce offspring, or ends with a last child that has no successor.
- Active motifs: completed youth and maturity `س و ي:B005/m01`; bodily constitution `ب ن ي:B002/m02`; infertility `ع ق ر:B004/m01`; last child or final instance `ع ق ر:B005/m01`; paired state `ز ك و:B005/m01`; placental birth-fluid `س ق ي:B005/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: The scene places successful maturation beside reproductive limit. A formed and paired body may generate descent, while infertility or a final child closes the line and turns completion into termination.

#### Subchannel C. Courtship, Unveiling, and Marital Compensation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A woman becomes available to suitors, enters marriage, is unveiled, and receives a gift or compensatory due.
- Active motifs: marriage and consummation `ب ن ي:B006/m01`; bridal unveiling `ج ل و:B003/m01`; bridal gift `ج ل و:B009/m01`; courted widow or divorcee `ر س ل:B009/m01`; intercourse `غ ش و:B004/m01` and `غ ش ي:B004/m01`; dower or sexual compensation `ع ق ر:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:3 `جلاها`; 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:5 `بناها`; 91:13 `رسول`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Courtship is a staged social transition. Availability opens negotiation, marriage changes household status, unveiling makes the bride publicly visible, and gift or compensation formalizes the exchange surrounding consummation.

### 11. Authority, Worship, and Covenant
- Semantic invariant: Legitimate or illegitimate authority establishes worship, differentiates ranks, binds parties, and enforces judgment.
- Surface relation: direct; 91:13 names `رسول الله` and `ناقة الله`, while 91:14 places judgment with `ربهم`.
- Surprising reach: Sacred authority extends lexically into scholars, local rulers, captains, deacons, treaties, prayer, sacrifice, and imprecation.

#### Subchannel A. Deity, Lordship, and Worship
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A deity is worshiped and a lord owns, governs, nurtures, and completes what is under that authority.
- Active motifs: deity and worship `ء ل ه:B001/m01`; lordship and ownership `ر ب ب:B001/m01`; nurture and completion `ر ب ب:B002/m01`; sacred house `ب ن ي:B003/m01`; self-protective reverence `و ق ي:B002/m01`; false object of worship `ط غ ي:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:13 two instances of `الله`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Worship and lordship establish a vertical relation of allegiance, care, and command. Protective reverence is the subject’s posture within that relation, while the false object of worship presents its rival and distortion.

#### Subchannel B. Institutional Rank and Guidance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Differentiated office-holders interpret, navigate, or direct a group within religious, political, or maritime institutions.
- Active motifs: local chieftain or authoritative speaker `ق و ل:B004/m01`; religious scholar `ر ب ب:B003/m01`; captain or pilot `ر ب ب:B017/m01`; deacon `ش م س:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Scholar, chieftain, captain, and deacon differ by institution, but each occupies a guiding rank. Their authority rests in interpretation, direction, or stewardship within an organized group.

#### Subchannel C. Covenant, Obligation, and Judgment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Parties enter a binding relation whose terms can be enforced, contested, or answered by consequence.
- Active motifs: covenant and pledged protection `ر ب ب:B011/m01`; guarantee or attached right `ت ل و:B004/m01`; substitute or commercial guarantee `ع ق ب:B010/m01`; objection or rebuttal `ع ق ب:B008/m01`; punishment after offense `ع ق ب:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: A covenant causes an obligation to follow its holder. Guarantee secures performance, objection tests the pledged term, and punishment supplies the enforcing consequence when the relation is violated.

#### Subchannel D. Invocation, Prayer, Sacrifice, and Imprecation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Sacred address invokes the divine name, seeks rain or blessing, offers sacrifice, or calls down harm.
- Active motifs: divine name in invocation or oath `ء ل ه:B002/m01`; prayer for rain `س ق ي:B006/m01`; sacrificial animal `ض ح و:B004/m01`; spoken address `ق و ل:B001/m01`; imprecation `ع ق ر:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `ضحاها`; 91:13 `قال`, two instances of `الله`, and `سقياها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Ritual speech acts in opposite directions: it requests provision and blessing, dedicates a sacrifice, or utters a curse. The divine name anchors each act as an address with consequences beyond ordinary conversation.

### 12. Protective Surfaces and Crafted Supports
- Semantic invariant: Material is configured as a cover, container, pad, string, or marked surface so that it can protect, carry, support, or display.
- Surface relation: indirect; the roots occur at 91:4 `يغشاها`, 91:5 `بناها`, 91:7 `سواها`, 91:8 `تقواها`, 91:13 `رسول`, and 91:15 `عقباها`.
- Surprising reach: Cosmic covering contracts into leather shelters and bags, while animal tendons, glued bows, dyed cloth, and deceptive patterns turn surfaces into tools and signs.

#### Subchannel A. Leather Shelter and Carrying Containers
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hide is stretched or sewn into a shelter, cover, garment, or carrying vessel.
- Active motifs: portable hide cover or container `ب ن ي:B004/m02`; protective barrier `و ق ي:B001/m01`; protective or carrying leather bag `خ و ف:B006/m01`; skin container for lots or arrows `ر ب ب:B010/m01`; hide-conditioning paste `ر ب ب:B006/m02`; saddle covering `غ ش و:B001/m02`; hide waterskin `ق م ر:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:5 `بناها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `يخاف`
- Synthesis: A single material system serves dwelling, transport, storage, and bodily protection. Stretching hide creates an enclosing surface; folding and sewing it convert that surface into a portable container.

#### Subchannel B. Tensioned Supports and Riding Gear
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Flexible material is tensioned or padded to transfer force without breaking the supported body or tool.
- Active motifs: bow adhering to its string `ب ن ي:B005/m01`; tendon used as bowstring or binding `ع ق ب:B001/m02`; riding pad `س و ي:B010/m01`; non-wounding saddle `و ق ي:B003/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: String, tendon, pad, and saddle all manage tension. Some store force for release, while others distribute weight and prevent injury; each works by maintaining a calibrated relation between flexible and rigid parts.

#### Subchannel C. Dye, Pattern, and Visible Surface
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Cloth receives color or pattern until its surface communicates status, beauty, or a deceptive appearance.
- Active motifs: cloth infused with dye `س ق ي:B009/m01`; small dyeing measure `ن ف س:B007/m01`; patterned cloth whose appearance misleads `ك ذ ب:B009/m01`; red garment `ع ق ر:B019/m01`; necklace pendants `ش م س:B004/m01`; white coloration `ق م ر:B002/m01`; veil or covering surface `غ ش و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:2 `القمر`; 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Dye penetrates a textile, ornament is attached to it, and the finished surface alters how the object is read. The patterned cloth’s “false” appearance makes visual display a material counterpart to verbal misrepresentation.

### 13. Deterioration, Affliction, and Cessation
- Semantic invariant: A living body, crop, container, or ongoing supply loses integrity, continuity, perception, or life.
- Surface relation: indirect; the roots occur at 91:2 `القمر`, 91:4 `يغشاها`, 91:6 `الأرض`, 91:10 `خاب`, 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`, 91:13 `سقياها`, and 91:14 `عقروها`.
- Surprising reach: Frosted dates, yellowing plants, termite-eaten wood, a spoiled waterskin, vanished milk, suppuration, and swoon all enact the same movement from viable continuity to breakdown.

#### Subchannel A. Ripening, Withering, and Crop Loss
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fruit begins to ripen, cold or cutting interrupts development, and plant tissue yellows or dries.
- Active motifs: fruit ripening from its tip `ذ ن ب:B005/m01`; dates spoiled by cold `ق م ر:B004/m01`; yellowing and drying plant `ع ق ب:B015/m01`; cut palm that withers `ع ق ر:B007/m01`; barren sand `ع ق ر:B017/m01`; productive plant growth `ز ك و:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `عقروها`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Development is tracked from the fruit’s tip through maturity toward possible loss. Cold, cutting, or barren ground interrupts the growth sequence, converting ripening into yellowing, dryness, and sterility.

#### Subchannel B. Material Decay and Failed Supply
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A container, wooden object, or recurring yield is corrupted until it no longer performs its function.
- Active motifs: termite consuming wood `ء ر ض:B010/m01`; moonlight-spoiled waterskin `ق م ر:B006/m01`; milk that disappears instead of continuing `ك ذ ب:B006/m01`; split or cut material `ف ل ح:B001/m01`; remnant after use `ع ق ب:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:9 `أفلح`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:15 `عقباها`
- Synthesis: Functional failure is gradual but observable: wood is eaten, hide separates or spoils, and an expected milk flow ceases. Splitting exposes the damaged structure, while the remnant records what survives after use or decay.

#### Subchannel C. Symptoms, Disease, and Loss of Awareness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Bodily disturbance becomes visible as tremor, cold, suppuration, fear, internal disease, or unconsciousness.
- Active motifs: bodily tremor `ء ر ض:B008/m01`; cold or catarrh `ء ر ض:B009/m01`; suppurating lesion `ء ر ض:B011/m01`; derangement `ء ر ض:B012/m01`; harmful gaze `ن ف س:B003/m01`; visible fear `خ و ف:B005/m01`; cleft lip `ف ل ح:B002/m01`; internal disease `غ ش ي:B003/m01`; habitual intoxicant `ع ق ر:B012/m01`; scorpion sting `ع ق ر:B020/m01`; swoon `غ ش و:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:9 `أفلح`; 91:14 `عقروها`; 91:15 `يخاف`
- Synthesis: Affliction is legible through altered movement, temperature, tissue, awareness, and expression. The progression moves from external symptom to internal seizure, ending when consciousness itself is covered.

#### Subchannel D. Breath, Blood, Last Gasp, and Consuming Death
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Vital breath and blood sustain life until injury or devouring affliction brings the final gasp.
- Active motifs: breath leaving the body `ن ف س:B001/m01`; life-blood `ن ف س:B004/m01`; living soul `ن ف س:B011/m01`; last gasp `ت ل و:B008/m01`; body-affecting drug or substance `ع ق ر:B008/m01`; consuming fever, calamity, or death `ل ه م:B005/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:8 `فألهمها`; 91:14 `عقروها`
- Synthesis: Life is represented through moving breath and circulating blood. Injury or consuming affliction disrupts those motions, and the last gasp marks the narrow boundary at which the living self departs.

### 14. Measure, Fitness, and Selection
- Semantic invariant: Objects, persons, claims, or actions are compared against a standard and found equal, fitting, mature, or chosen.
- Surface relation: indirect; the roots occur at 91:5 `السماء`, 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`, 91:8 `تقواها`, 91:9 `زكاها`, 91:13 `رسول` and `ناقة`, and 91:15 `عقباها`.
- Surprising reach: Abstract parity becomes concrete in an ounce, a paired game-call, a fair midpoint, meticulous handling, and selection from alternatives.

#### Subchannel A. Equality, Standard, and Fair Middle
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Two things are compared, balanced, or located against a shared standard.
- Active motifs: equality and equivalence `س و ي:B001/m01`; fair midpoint `س و ي:B006/m01`; measured ounce `و ق ي:B004/m01`; wealth calibrated to a head `س و ي:B013/m01`; diminution by taking `خ و ف:B004/m01`; small portion `ط غ و:B007/m01`; fitting equivalence `ز ك و:B004/m01`; parity in name `س م و:B005/m02`; corresponding gap `ع ق ر:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `السماء`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:11 `طغواها`; 91:14 `عقروها`; 91:15 `يخاف`
- Synthesis: Equality can be quantitative, spatial, verbal, or nominal. Weight supplies a fixed standard, the midpoint supplies a fair position, and naming or fit supplies equivalence at the level of social and linguistic classification.

#### Subchannel B. Suitability, Deliberation, and Meticulous Handling
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An action is slowed and refined until it suits the object, circumstance, or norm.
- Active motifs: aptitude for good `ء ر ض:B003/m01`; suitability and propriety `ز ك و:B004/m01`; deliberation and composure `ر س ل:B004/m01`; meticulous handling `ن و ق:B005/m01`; straight finished form `س و ي:B002/m01`; sustained concern `ق و ل:B015/m01`; respite and room `ن ف س:B015/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:13 `قال`, `رسول`, and `ناقة`
- Synthesis: Fitness is produced through attention rather than speed. Deliberation creates room to assess, meticulous handling adjusts the action, and a straight finished form shows that the result suits its intended condition.

#### Subchannel C. Choice, Pairing, and Category Shift
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An item is selected or paired, while a proverb exposes a mismatch or transformation of category.
- Active motifs: careful selection `ن و ق:B006/m01`; pair or even state `ز ك و:B005/m01`; otherness and substitution `س و ي:B007/m01`; chosen designation `س م و:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `السماء`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:13 `ناقة`
- Synthesis: Selection presupposes alternatives and a criterion of fit. Pairing confirms compatibility, otherness marks the rejected alternative, and designation fixes the chosen item within a named class.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Casting Lots and Gaming Implements
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Players assemble lots and implements, cast or strike them, and compete for a winning result.
- Active motifs: gambling contest `ق م ر:B007/m01`; leather bag holding lots `ر ب ب:B010/m01`; fifth gaming arrow `ن ف س:B016/m01`; game bat `ق و ل:B008/m01`; paired game-call `ز ك و:B005/m02`; short arrow `ر س ل:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:9 `زكاها`; 91:10 `خاب`; 91:13 `قال` and `رسول`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: The scene joins equipment, storage, chance, and rivalry. Lots or arrows are kept together, a turn selects or strikes an implement, and the resulting combination determines advantage or defeat.

### S2. Naming and Proper-Name Transfer
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A descriptive word becomes a name, title, invocation, or place-name and then circulates independently of its original image.
- Active motifs: naming and designation `س م و:B005/m01`; solar-derived names `ش م س:B007/m01`; proper names from day or river vocabulary `ن ه ر:B007/m01`; Layla as a personal name `ل ي ل:B004/m01`; divine name in invocation `ء ل ه:B002/m01`; named valley associated with falsehood `خ ي ب:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `الشمس`; 91:3 `النهار`; 91:4 `الليل`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:10 `خاب`; 91:13 two instances of `الله`
- Synthesis: Naming detaches a lexical image from its immediate scene and fixes it to a person, place, or sacred address. Sun, day, night, valley, and divine invocation thus become sources of durable social reference.

### S3. Constitution, Essence, and Logical Definition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A thing is understood through its composed form, intrinsic identity, signification, and verbal boundary.
- Active motifs: composed constitution `ب ن ي:B002/m01`; essence or intrinsic self `ن ف س:B012/m02`; signification `ق و ل:B014/m01`; logical definition `ق و ل:B016/m01`; otherness `س و ي:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:5 `بناها`; 91:7 `نفس` and `سواها`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:14 `فسواها`
- Synthesis: Constitution describes how a thing is put together, essence what it is in itself, and definition how speech marks its boundary. Otherness supplies the contrast needed to distinguish that identity from substitutes.

### S4. Exile, Strangerhood, and Night Travel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person leaves the homeland, becomes a stranger, and travels or pursues a route under reduced visibility.
- Active motifs: expulsion or departure from homeland `ج ل و:B004/m01`; stranger as “son of the land” `ء ر ض:B004/m01`; extended wandering `ط ح و:B002/m01`; night travel and activity `ل ي ل:B002/m01`; moonlit approach `ق م ر:B003/m02`; easy traveling gait `ر س ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:2 `القمر`; 91:3 `جلاها`; 91:4 `الليل`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:13 `رسول`
- Synthesis: Departure breaks the relation between person and homeland, producing strangerhood as a social state. Night and moonlight shape the journey’s visibility, while gait and pursuit give the displaced traveler a directed path through unfamiliar ground.

### S5. Supposition, Modality, and Otherness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A proposition is framed as uncertain, directed toward an alternative, or contrasted with what is other.
- Active motifs: saying used as supposition `ق و ل:B011/m01`; particle of qualification or possibility `ر ب ب:B015/m01`; otherness and substitution `س و ي:B007/m01`; directed reference `س و ي:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:14 `ربهم`
- Synthesis: Modality changes how a statement relates to reality without changing its subject. Qualification lowers certainty, otherness opens an alternative, and directed reference locates the proposition relative to a distinct object or belief.

### S6. Predawn and Forenoon Meals
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Eating and drinking are organized around the predawn and forenoon intervals.
- Active motifs: predawn meal `ف ل ح:B006/m01`; forenoon meal `ض ح و:B003/m01`; current night `ل ي ل:B003/m01`; bright day interval `ن ه ر:B002/m01`; measured draught `ن ف س:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:1 `ضحاها`; 91:3 `النهار`; 91:4 `الليل`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:9 `أفلح`
- Synthesis: The meals are distinguished by their temporal placement. Night leads to the sustaining predawn intake, day advances toward the forenoon meal, and the draught supplies the small bodily rhythm within each eating interval.

### S7. Attendance and Visitation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A visitor approaches a person or place, enters the social field, and is received with familiarity or confrontation.
- Active motifs: coming to a person or place `غ ش و:B003/m01` and `غ ش ي:B005/m01`; turning toward a destination `س و ي:B004/m01`; familiarity and ease with another `ر س ل:B007/m01`; exposing oneself in confrontation `ء ر ض:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 91:4 `يغشاها`; 91:6 `الأرض`; 91:7 and 91:14 `سواها`; 91:13 `رسول`
- Synthesis: Visitation begins as directed approach and becomes a social relation at arrival. Familiar reception permits ease and continued attendance, while confrontation turns the same face-to-face exposure into challenge.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s091/surah.r2.hftbundle/hft.md =====
# HFT: earlier activation hypotheses, per focus ayah of surah 91

Note: HFT used an older root map; a trace step on a root the gateway now withholds is an echo, not identity.

# Focus 91:1

## radiant extension
- reading: An oath by a luminous source and by the temporal-spatial unfolding of its clear reach.
- mechanism: The sun supplies the radiating source, while ḍuḥā supplies brightness extended across a rising interval. The focus therefore presents illumination as an unfolding reach rather than a static object.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: The literal sun and its radiating daylight supply the source from which the focus mechanism extends.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B001: The rising, extended forenoon supplies duration and turns the sun's light into an unfolding interval.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B005: Clear ḍuḥā brightness supplies the qualitative clarity carried through that interval.

## exposure event
- reading: The verse names a source together with its act of placing a field openly under light and heat.
- mechanism: Radiance moves outward until bodies and actions occupy an exposed, public surface. The paired terms thus describe an event of manifestation: a source brings what is outside into visibility.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: Solar light supplies the physical agent of manifestation.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Exposure to heat, outward prominence, and public visibility supply the manifested field produced by the sun.

## measured ripening
- reading: Ḍuḥā is a measured maturation of visibility, with delay functioning as part of how clarity forms.
- mechanism: The light reaches fullness by measured continuation. This allows the focus to carry a tempo: manifestation ripens through delay rather than arriving as an instantaneous flash.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B001: The forenoon's rising extension supplies a process that takes time to reach fullness.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B006: Gentle delay supplies the pacing rule by which the illumination unfolds without haste.

## creaturely provision
- reading: The sun's ḍuḥā is a usable life interval in which creatures feed, graze, and receive provision.
- mechanism: The sunlight interval organizes embodied access to food rather than merely illuminating scenery. The possessive pairing can therefore be heard as the sun's creature-serving turn or usable allotment.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: Daylight supplies the environmental condition in which the provision interval operates.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B003: Forenoon feeding and early grazing supply the creaturely use that makes the interval materially consequential.

## guarded restiveness
- reading: The sun is also a difficult, guarded power whose outward exposure makes its force impossible to keep private.
- mechanism: A force that resists capture or guards what lies behind it nevertheless becomes publicly manifest through its ḍuḥā. This yields a tense rather than placid solar image: withheld power declares itself by exposure.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B002: Restive refusal supplies a force that will not settle or submit to being ridden.
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B006: Guarding and withholding supply the protected interior or reserve carried by that force.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Open prominence supplies the contrary motion by which the guarded or unruly force becomes visible.

## leading succession
- reading: The sun's ḍuḥā leads a luminous succession and leaves a phase that can be followed or carried onward.
- mechanism: The moon's own light is explicitly placed in following relation to the sun. Followership and a remainder that comes after strengthen the baseline extension model: ḍuḥā is the leading phase of a train, not an isolated span.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B001: Extended forenoon supplies the leading duration from which a succession can proceed.
  - 91:2 **وَٱلْقَمَرِ** ق م ر B001: The moon and its sky-light supply the distinct luminous follower.
  - 91:2 **تَلَىٰهَا** ت ل و B001: Following and serial continuation supply the directional link from solar phase to lunar phase.
  - 91:2 **تَلَىٰهَا** ت ل و B003: A remainder following what precedes supplies an afterlight or residual phase rather than mere adjacency.

## reciprocal disclosure
- reading: Solar clarity is relational: it opens the day, while the opened day in turn reveals and sharpens the sun.
- mechanism: Day is an opening field, and its revealing or polishing action makes the sun manifest. The apparent source of visibility is thus also disclosed by a medium that it helps create, producing reciprocal rather than one-way illumination.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: The radiating sun supplies the apparent source whose independence is being revised.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B005: Clear brightness supplies the quality that must itself become perceptible in a field.
  - 91:3 **وَٱلنَّهَارِ** ن ه ر B002: Day opening through light supplies the receiving field in which the sun appears.
  - 91:3 **وَٱلنَّهَارِ** ن ه ر B003: Opening and widening supply the spatial expansion that gives radiance room to become manifest.
  - 91:3 **جَلَّىٰهَا** ج ل و B001: Uncovering and appearance supply the day-to-sun direction of disclosure.
  - 91:3 **جَلَّىٰهَا** ج ل و B002: Polishing supplies a material analogy in which the medium intensifies the source's legibility.

## reversible legibility
- reading: Ḍuḥā is one phase in a reversible contest between disclosure and overlay, with a contained possibility that seeing and understanding rise and fall together.
- mechanism: The exposed field can be overlaid by darkness, while the non-dominant mapped inventory extends covering from surfaces to understanding. The focus's manifestation model therefore becomes reversible physical and cognitive legibility.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Open visibility supplies the legible state that can subsequently be covered.
  - 91:4 **وَٱلَّيْلِ** ل ي ل B001: Night and its darkness supply the alternating condition opposed to exposed ḍuḥā.
  - 91:4 **يَغْشَىٰهَا** غ ش و B001: A covering laid over and concealing a thing supplies the physical reversal of manifestation.
  - 91:4 **يَغْشَىٰهَا** غ ش و B007: Covering of understanding supplies the exploratory epistemic analogue of the same reversal.

## built luminous field
- reading: The sun's ḍuḥā appears as a mark borne and stabilized by a built luminous field.
- mechanism: The sky is both an overhead enclosure and, through a non-dominant mapped root, a surface carrying a visible mark; building joins parts and supports. Solar exposure can thus be read as a legible event inside a constructed field rather than light poured into emptiness.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: Solar radiance supplies the event placed within the contextual architecture.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Visible prominence supplies the legible appearance produced inside that field.
  - 91:5 **وَٱلسَّمَآءِ** س م و B004: The overhead sky supplies the enclosing field in which solar disclosure occurs.
  - 91:5 **وَٱلسَّمَآءِ** س م و B001: A visible identifying mark supplies the secondary mapped image of radiance as inscription or sign.
  - 91:5 **بَنَىٰهَا** ب ن ي B001: Joining parts into a construction supplies the assembly relation behind the luminous field.
  - 91:5 **بَنَىٰهَا** ب ن ي B009: Ribs and supports supply the material image of a field held in a stable configuration.

## ground laid by light
- reading: Ḍuḥā also lays out a usable world: temporal illumination becomes the opening of fertile, creature-accessible space.
- mechanism: The extension first heard temporally in ḍuḥā is repeated materially as earth spread outward. Fertile ground gives that spread a life-bearing use, so illumination can be read as laying out a habitable surface for action.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B001: Temporal extension supplies the focus-side motion that the earth later translates into space.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B003: Early grazing supplies the embodied use that connects sunlight to habitable ground.
  - 91:6 **وَٱلْأَرْضِ** ء ر ض B002: Soft fertile earth supplies the life-bearing surface opened under the light.
  - 91:6 **طَحَىٰهَا** ط ح و B001: Spreading and extending supply the horizontal counterpart to ḍuḥā's temporal reach.

## inward diagnostic light
- reading: The sun's ḍuḥā also models an inward disclosure under which a formed self can recognize both its cover-breaking and self-protecting capacities.
- mechanism: The oath turns from exposed cosmos to an inward self with reason and character, formed to completion and made to contain opposed capacities. Breaching a cover and warding harm convert ḍuḥā's visibility into a diagnostic light by which inner directions become distinguishable.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Open appearance supplies the focus-side operation transferred from outer surfaces to inward discernment.
  - 91:7 **وَنَفْسٍ** ن ف س B013: Reason and inward awareness supply the interior field that can become legible.
  - 91:7 **وَنَفْسٍ** ن ف س B014: Inner force and character supply dispositions whose directions need disclosure.
  - 91:7 **سَوَّىٰهَا** س و ي B002: Integrity and completion supply the formed container in which opposed capacities can coexist.
  - 91:8 **فَأَلْهَمَهَا** ل ه م B001: Whole ingestion supplies an exploratory image of the paired capacities becoming inward contents rather than external labels.
  - 91:8 **فُجُورَهَا** ف ج ر B004: Deviation and breaching a cover supply the outward-breaking direction exposed within the self.
  - 91:8 **وَتَقْوَىٰهَا** و ق ي B001: Warding off harm supplies the opposed protective direction exposed within the same self.

## cultivated radiance
- reading: Ḍuḥā participates in making the field fruitful: exposure, work, nourishment, growth, and purification form a shared transformation circuit.
- mechanism: Spread fertile ground is worked by cutting it, then answers with growth and purification. The forenoon's grazing interval becomes one phase of a cultivation circuit in which exposure enables productive transformation rather than merely revealing what is already there.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: Sunlight supplies the energy-bearing environmental condition for the cultivation model.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B003: Early grazing and feeding supply the focus-side evidence that illumination enters a nourishment cycle.
  - 91:6 **وَٱلْأَرْضِ** ء ر ض B002: Fertile earth supplies the substrate capable of answering exposure with life.
  - 91:6 **طَحَىٰهَا** ط ح و B001: Spreading supplies the preparation of the substrate as an available field.
  - 91:9 **أَفْلَحَ** ف ل ح B003: The cultivator who cleaves earth supplies the intervention that converts an open field into productive ground.
  - 91:9 **زَكَّىٰهَا** ز ك و B001: Growth and increase supply the material result of the cultivation circuit.
  - 91:9 **زَكَّىٰهَا** ز ك و B002: Purity and soundness supply the ethical result that coexists with material growth.

## anti duha burial
- reading: Ḍuḥā becomes the positive direction of activated manifestation, while self-burial is its corrupting reversal: an anti-ḍuḥā.
- mechanism: A fire-making implement that fails to kindle and a movement into hiddenness reverse both solar radiance and ḍuḥā's open prominence. Failure becomes not mere lack of light but the active burial and corruption of a capacity that could have appeared.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B001: Radiating daylight supplies the positive condition negated by an implement that will not kindle.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Open visibility supplies the positive direction reversed by concealment.
  - 91:10 **خَابَ** خ ي ب B002: A fire-striker that fails to produce flame supplies the image of latent brightness that never activates.
  - 91:10 **دَسَّىٰهَا** د س و B001: Secret insertion or burial supplies the downward, hidden motion opposed to ḍuḥā's emergence.
  - 91:10 **دَسَّىٰهَا** د س و B003: Corruption supplies the degrading effect of sustained concealment.

## visibility not truth
- reading: Ḍuḥā makes things public but does not compel true seeing; radiance can become glare, and a fully visible surface can still deceive.
- mechanism: Blinding whiteness, an appearance that lies by its condition, and force overflowing its limit weaken any simple equation of brightness with truth. Exposure guarantees publicity, but saturation or deceptive surface can still defeat discernment.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Public visibility supplies the baseline condition now separated from truthfulness.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B005: Clear brightness supplies the apparent guarantee that the context complicates.
  - 91:2 **وَٱلْقَمَرِ** ق م ر B005: Vision bewildered by intense whiteness supplies the perceptual failure caused by excessive brightness.
  - 91:11 **كَذَّبَتْ** ك ذ ب B009: A garment whose appearance misstates its condition supplies deceptive surface despite visibility.
  - 91:11 **بِطَغْوَىٰهَآ** ط غ ي B002: Overflowing water supplies the saturation mechanism by which an otherwise useful force exceeds discernible bounds.

## ambivalent rising
- reading: Rising is morally ambivalent activation: a latent force may enter full prominence as illumination or as destructive transgression.
- mechanism: The focus's restive force and open prominence are activated by transgression, the stirring of what was still, and a non-dominant image of a rising mountain ridge attached to the worst actor. Emergence into prominence is therefore energetic but not automatically good.
- trace:
  - 91:1 **وَٱلشَّمْسِ** ش م س B002: Restive refusal supplies an unruly force capable of activation.
  - 91:1 **وَضُحَىٰهَا** ض ح و B002: Visible prominence supplies the upward or outward arrival of that force.
  - 91:11 **بِطَغْوَىٰهَآ** ط غ ي B001: Boundary-crossing disobedience supplies the direction taken by the activated force.
  - 91:12 **ٱنۢبَعَثَ** ب ع ث B001: Stirring something from stillness supplies the transition from latency to action.
  - 91:12 **أَشْقَىٰهَا** ش ق و B004: The secondary mapped image of a tall rising mountain ridge supplies the strange but visible prominence of the worst actor.

## protected allotment
- reading: Its ḍuḥā can also be heard as a due and usable turn within a life-supporting order, protected from both deprivation and monopolizing restraint.
